  • Exaggerated: --hole-ladder 1. Only the very quietest step may be cut, so nearly every band survives and the whole budget problem turns into coarser quantisation. On very dense material at 24 kbps this can sound uniformly gritty: a caricature of the trade.

Works with / against: combines well with --avoid-holes 1 and --hole-start. The rate loop can only raise the step so far; at extreme budgets bands still vanish because they quantise to zero on their own, which no switch here prevents.

Measured (dense48, LC stereo, holes after decoding): 32 kbps / 17 kHz: 37% to 8% with 2; 40 kbps / 18 kHz: 37% to 1.7%; 50 kbps / 19 kHz: 35% to 0.2%. The error-to-signal ratio in the bands that remain gets 0.3..0.5 dB worse.

Under the hood: the limit on enIdx in the "allow more holes" loop of adj_thr.cpp.

--avoid-holes <0|1>

Forces FDK's peak and valley protection on or off.

  • Applies to: the AAC core in every profile.
  • Values: 0 off, 1 on; default: off, which is stock behaviour (on from 20 kbps per channel, off below)
  • Input: whole number, 0 or 1

Before cutting bands, FDK can relax the SNR it demands in a smarter way: it lowers the demand in valleys between spectral peaks and keeps it in the peaks, so that narrow loud bands survive and the savings come from where the ear notices them least. Stock enables this only from 20 kbps per channel upwards, which for stereo means from 40 kbps. Below that, right inside the 24..50 kbps range this group is aimed at, the protection is off and cutting starts earlier.

Values:

  • 1: protection on at every bitrate. Below 40 kbps stereo this is the meaningful setting; at or above it nothing changes, because stock already has it on.
  • 0: protection off at every bitrate. A diagnostic setting: it shows what the protection was buying you.

Example in context: -p 2 -b 32000 -w 17000 --avoid-holes 1 --hole-ladder 2.

Works with / against: on its own the effect is small (measured), because the protection only reorders where the savings come from; it helps most together with --hole-ladder.

Measured: at LC 40 and 128 kbps stereo --avoid-holes 1 gives a stream identical to stock (already on there), --avoid-holes 0 changes it.

--hole-start <sfb>

Protects the lower part of the spectrum in long blocks from being cut to zero.

  • Applies to: the AAC core, long blocks (LC, the HE-AAC core, LD, ELD).
  • Values: scalefactor band 0..51; default: off, which is stock behaviour (band 15 when the protection is on, band 0 when it is off)
  • Input: whole number

Bands below this number are never chosen as holes. Stock starts at band 15, which at 48 kHz is roughly 2 to 3 kHz: the bass and the low mids are safe, everything above may be cut. Raising the number moves the protected region up; lowering it allows holes deeper down. Remember that a protected band still costs bits: the savings then have to come from the bands above, or from coarser quantisation everywhere.

How many bands exist depends on profile and sample rate; --verbose prints the count as active SFBs (long), 49 for LC stereo at 48 kHz.

Examples, protecting more:

  • Mild: --hole-start 20. A few more bands in the low mids are safe.
  • Strong: --hole-start 25. Protection up to roughly the presence region; measured to cut holes from 37% to 26% at 32 kbps.
  • Exaggerated: --hole-start 51. No band in a long block may be cut by this mechanism at all. The whole budget problem lands on the quantiser; at low bitrate the top gets very coarse, and bands can still vanish by quantising to zero.

Examples, allowing deeper holes:

  • Mild: --hole-start 12. A little more freedom just below the stock start.
  • Strong: --hole-start 6. Holes allowed down into the low mids.
  • Exaggerated: --hole-start 0. Even the bass bands may be cut. Useful only to hear what the stock protection prevents.

Short blocks: without --hole-start-short the same frequency is used there (the nearest short band edge; --hole-start 25, 5 kHz at 48 kHz, becomes short band 6, and the stock pair 15/3 maps onto itself). --verbose shows it.

Works with / against: --hole-start-short sets the short value explicitly and wins.

--hole-start-short <sfb>

The same protection for short blocks.

  • Applies to: LC and the HE-AAC core, frames coded as short blocks. LD and ELD have none.
  • Values: short-block scalefactor band 0..15; default: off, which is stock behaviour (band 3), or the frequency of --hole-start when that one is given
  • Input: whole number

A short block has about 14 bands at 44.1 or 48 kHz, so each band is wide and the numbers are small. Stock protects bands 0..2.

Examples, protecting more:

  • Mild: --hole-start-short 5.
  • Strong: --hole-start-short 8. Most of the band in a transient frame is protected.
  • Exaggerated: --hole-start-short 15. No short band may be cut; transient frames get coarse instead.

Examples, allowing deeper holes:

  • Mild: --hole-start-short 2.
  • Strong: --hole-start-short 1.
  • Exaggerated: --hole-start-short 0. The attack frame may lose its bass.

--hole-spread <dB>

Decides how readily a band counts as covered by its neighbours, and therefore as a safe candidate for a hole.

  • Applies to: the AAC core in every profile.
  • Values: 0.0..24.0 dB; default: off, which is stock behaviour (3 dB for long blocks, 2 dB for short blocks)
  • Input: decimal, up to 3 places, . or ,

A band may be cut more safely when its neighbours mask it anyway. To judge that, FDK compares the band's energy with the energy spread into it from neighbouring bands, after lowering that spread energy by a margin. A small margin makes neighbours look strong, so many bands look covered and become candidates. A large margin makes neighbours look weak, so fewer bands look covered and more of them are protected.

This is a fine knob. On our material it moved the hole count by one or two percent of bands, far less than --hole-ladder.

Examples, protecting more:

  • Mild: --hole-spread 4.5.
  • Strong: --hole-spread 9.
  • Exaggerated: --hole-spread 24. Neighbours practically never count as covering, so almost no band qualifies on that ground.

Examples, allowing more holes:

  • Mild: --hole-spread 2.
  • Strong: --hole-spread 1.
  • Exaggerated: --hole-spread 0. The spread energy is taken at face value; any band sitting next to louder ones is a candidate.

The value is for long blocks; short blocks get two thirds of it, which keeps the stock ratio (3 dB long, 2 dB short). --hole-spread 3 is therefore exactly stock in both. Up to 1.4.0-beta.1 one value replaced both.

--preecho-rise <dB>

Limits how fast the masking threshold of a band may grow from one frame to the next, so that quantisation noise cannot run ahead of an attack in a long block.

  • Applies to: long blocks in every profile. Most relevant in LD and ELD, and in LC with long blocks forced (--block-bias 0).
  • Values: 0.0..24.0 dB; default: off, which is stock behaviour (3.01 dB, a factor of 2 in power)
  • Input: decimal, up to 3 places, . or ,

The psychoacoustic model computes a masking threshold per band and frame. In a frame that contains an attack, the threshold jumps up, because the attack masks a lot. But the noise of that frame also sits before the attack, where nothing masks it. Pre-echo control therefore caps the rise: this frame's threshold may be at most N dB above the previous frame's. Below 3.01 the cap is tighter (less pre-echo, more bits on onsets); above it the cap is looser (fewer bits, more pre-echo).

In LC the effect is usually small, because real attacks are already moved into short blocks before this cap matters. LD and ELD have no short blocks, so there this switch and TNS are the main defence.

Examples, tighter:

  • Mild: --preecho-rise 2.
  • Strong: --preecho-rise 1. Onsets in long blocks get noticeably more bits.
  • Exaggerated: --preecho-rise 0. A threshold may never rise at all from frame to frame. Every crescendo is coded as if it were still quiet, which wastes bits on loud passages and starves everything else under CBR.

Examples, looser:

  • Mild: --preecho-rise 4.5.
  • Strong: --preecho-rise 9.
  • Exaggerated: --preecho-rise 24. The cap is practically gone: pre-echo control is off.

Works with / against: --preecho-depth limits the opposite side of the same rule.

Measured: tenths of a dB of pre-echo energy in LC on attacks48 (the attacks already go to short blocks). The switch changes LD and ELD streams.

--preecho-depth <dB>

Limits how far pre-echo control may lower a threshold at most.

  • Applies to: the same as --preecho-rise.
  • Values: 0.0..60.0 dB (the effect stops growing near 45 dB); default: off, which is stock behaviour (20 dB)
  • Input: decimal, up to 3 places, . or ,

When the rise cap pulls a threshold down, this switch sets the floor: the threshold may not end up more than N dB below what the psychoacoustic model computed for the frame. It keeps the cap from demanding absurd precision after a long silence, when the previous frame's threshold was tiny.

Examples, allowing deeper lowering:

  • Mild: --preecho-depth 25.
  • Strong: --preecho-depth 40. After silence, the first attack is coded very cleanly and very expensively.
  • Exaggerated: --preecho-depth 60. Practically no floor; under CBR a single onset after a pause can eat the reservoir. The internal factor bottoms out near 45 dB, so values between about 45 and 60 behave the same.

Examples, shallower:

  • Mild: --preecho-depth 15.
  • Strong: --preecho-depth 6.
  • Exaggerated: --preecho-depth 0. A threshold may not be lowered at all, which switches pre-echo control off regardless of --preecho-rise.

--attack-decay <x>

Sets the memory of the attack detector that chooses between long and short blocks.

  • Applies to: LC and the HE-AAC core (the profiles with short blocks). In our test it also had no effect on LD or ELD.
  • Values: 0.0..0.99; default: off, which is stock behaviour (0.70)
  • Input: decimal, up to 3 places, . or ,

The detector splits each frame into sub-windows, filters out the low frequencies, and compares the energy of each sub-window with a running average of what came before. A large enough ratio is an attack. The running average keeps a share of its value per sub-window: that share is this switch. Higher values mean a long memory, so an attack is compared with a long average; lower values mean the comparison is with the last moment.

A long memory is calmer and more selective on dense material, but after a loud passage the average stays high and an attack that follows can be missed. A short memory catches attacks right after loud passages, but on busy material it can fire too often and put tonal frames into short blocks, which costs bits and resolution.

Examples, longer memory:

  • Mild: --attack-decay 0.8.
  • Strong: --attack-decay 0.9. Measured: worst case pre-echo 2.9 dB lower on attacks48.
  • Exaggerated: --attack-decay 0.99. The average barely moves; attacks after loud passages are often missed.

Examples, shorter memory:

  • Mild: --attack-decay 0.6.
  • Strong: --attack-decay 0.4.
  • Exaggerated: --attack-decay 0. Each sub-window is compared only with the one before; the detector becomes jumpy on noisy material.

--attack-floor <dB>

Shifts the minimum energy an attack must have before it counts.

  • Applies to: LC and the HE-AAC core. In our test it also changed an LD stream, not ELD.
  • Values: -60.0..30.0 dB; default: off, which is stock behaviour (0 dB shift)
  • Input: decimal, up to 3 places, . or ,

Besides the ratio, the detector requires an absolute minimum energy, so that ticks in near-silence do not trigger short blocks. Negative values lower that floor: quiet attacks such as brushes, soft hi-hats or a quiet guitar pick also switch to short blocks. Positive values raise it: only loud attacks count.

Examples, catching quieter attacks:

  • Mild: --attack-floor -6.
  • Strong: --attack-floor -20.
  • Exaggerated: --attack-floor -60. Practically any rise in a quiet passage counts. Expect short blocks on room noise and on soft tonal passages, with the bit cost that brings.

Examples, only loud attacks:

  • Mild: --attack-floor 6.
  • Strong: --attack-floor 15.
  • Exaggerated: --attack-floor 30. Soft percussion stays in long blocks and pre-echo appears on it.

--window-shape <0|1>

Chooses the window shape of every block: sine everywhere or KBD everywhere.

  • Applies to: AAC-LC and the LC core of HE-AAC and HE-AAC v2. LD and ELD use a fixed window and are not affected.
  • Values: 0 sine, 1 KBD; default: off, which is stock behaviour (KBD for long blocks, sine for short blocks)
  • Input: whole number, 0 or 1

The window decides how the energy of a block leaks into neighbouring frequencies. A sine window has a narrow main lobe (good selectivity next to a peak) and slowly falling side lobes (energy and noise leak far). A KBD window has a wider main lobe and side lobes that fall much faster. In time the same property means that KBD keeps quantisation noise closer to where it belongs. Stock uses sine for short blocks, which are exactly the blocks around attacks.

The choice costs one bit per frame and is legal everywhere in LC; the stream carries the shape actually used, also in short blocks, and every decoder applies it.

Values:

  • 1: KBD in every block. Measured: pre-echo lower by 3.4..6.1 dB on average and by 1.2..6.6 dB in the worst case at 24, 40 and 128 kbps. The best single switch against pre-echo in LC.
  • 0: sine in every block. Better selectivity next to strong tonal peaks in long blocks, more leakage; mainly a comparison setting.

Example in context: -p 2 -b 40000 -w 18000 --window-shape 1.

--short-groups <0..3>

Chooses how the eight short windows of a transient frame are grouped.

  • Applies to: LC and the HE-AAC core, frames coded as short blocks.
  • Values: 0..3; default: off, which is stock behaviour (the table FDK picks by attack position)
  • Input: whole number

A transient frame consists of eight short windows. Windows in the same group share one set of scalefactors, so fewer groups means less side information and more bits for the spectrum, but coarser control of the noise in time. More groups means the noise can be shaped window by window, at a price.

Values:

  • 0: the stock table (the same as leaving the switch off).
  • 1: one group for all eight windows. The cheapest; the noise is spread over the whole frame, which partly undoes what the short blocks were for.
  • 2: four pairs. Even time resolution across the frame. Measured: the fewest holes in short frames on our material.
  • 3: tight around the attack: the attack window alone, one window on each side of it, the rest lumped. The most bits where the attack is.

Example in context: -p 2 -b 32000 -w 17000 --short-groups 2.

--tns-thresh <x>

Changes how readily TNS switches on.

  • Applies to: every profile with a core: LC, the HE-AAC core, LD, ELD.
  • Values: 0.5..4.0, a multiplier; default: off, which is stock behaviour (1.0)
  • Input: decimal, up to 3 places, . or ,

TNS (temporal noise shaping) runs a prediction filter across frequency. Where the signal has a strong time envelope, such as an attack inside a block, the filter makes the quantisation noise follow that envelope in time, so the noise sits under the loud part instead of in front of it. FDK enables TNS in a frame only when the prediction gain is high enough to be worth the side information. This switch multiplies that required gain. Below 1 TNS is used in more frames; above 1 in fewer.

In LC, short blocks already handle the sharpest attacks, so TNS covers the softer ones. In LD and ELD, which have no short blocks, TNS is the main tool against pre-echo, and a lower threshold there is often worth trying first.

Examples, more TNS:

  • Mild: --tns-thresh 0.85.
  • Strong: --tns-thresh 0.7. Measured: in LC at low bitrate the extra side information leaves more holes.
  • Exaggerated: --tns-thresh 0.5. TNS on almost everywhere, also where there is nothing to shape; bits go to filter coefficients.

Examples, less TNS:

  • Mild: --tns-thresh 1.3.
  • Strong: --tns-thresh 2. Fewer bits on side information, slightly fewer holes, more pre-echo on soft attacks.
  • Exaggerated: --tns-thresh 4. TNS practically off.

Works with / against: --tns-mask and --tns-order (group D) decide whether and how large the filter may be; this switch decides how often it is worth it.

--tns-start <Hz>

Sets the lowest frequency the lower TNS filter covers.

  • Applies to: AAC-LC and the LC core of HE-AAC, long and short blocks. Measured no effect in LD and ELD.
  • Values: 0..24000 Hz; default: off, which is stock behaviour (8 scalefactor bands, about 1.4 kHz at 48 kHz)
  • Input: whole number

Stock keeps the lowest bands out of TNS in long blocks and starts at 0 Hz in short ones. The value is rounded to the nearest scalefactor band edge of each table, and it cannot go above the top of the TNS range. Up to 1.4.0-beta.1 short blocks ignored it.

Examples, reaching lower:

  • Mild: --tns-start 1000.
  • Strong: --tns-start 400. TNS also shapes the noise of low mids, useful on piano and plucked bass notes.
  • Exaggerated: --tns-start 0. TNS covers the spectrum from the bottom, including the bass bands, where its side benefit is small and its errors can show as low frequency "breathing".

Examples, starting higher:

  • Mild: --tns-start 2000.
  • Strong: --tns-start 5000.
  • Exaggerated: --tns-start 15000. Only the very top is shaped; for most material TNS is then effectively off in long blocks.

--coverage <-5..5>

One axis at a fixed bitrate: code every band coarsely, or fewer bands cleanly.

  • Applies to: AAC core (LC, LD, ELD and the LC core of HE-AAC). Off by default.
  • Values: whole numbers -5..5; 0 = stock
  • Input: whole number

At a fixed bitrate the encoder can spend its bits on covering the whole spectrum with a coarse description, or on describing fewer bands well and leaving holes. This macro moves that balance with one number. It sets the six switches below (--band-knee, --quant-round, --thr-hold, --scf-range and, at +4 and +5, --pns-above); any of them given explicitly wins over the macro. --verbose prints the values in effect.

Measured at LC 32 kbps, 17 kHz, after decoding (zeroed bands, and noise of the bands that were kept): -5 51% holes with the kept bands 4.75 dB cleaner than the signal; stock 11.7% holes, 1.96 dB; +4 0.2% holes, with the kept bands about as noisy as the content.

Examples:

  • Mild, more coverage: --coverage 2. Fewer holes, a little more grain.
  • Strong, more coverage: --coverage 4. Practically no holes; adds noise substitution from 11 kHz up.
  • Exaggerated, more coverage: --coverage 5. Everything filled, noise from 7 kHz up.
  • Mild, more precision: --coverage -2. The weakest bands leave first.
  • Strong, more precision: --coverage -4. Many holes, what stays is clean.
  • Exaggerated, more precision: --coverage -5. About half of the bands gone.

--band-knee <dB>

Shift every band's margin above its threshold near the coded/zeroed edge, in every channel.

  • Applies to: AAC core. Off by default.
  • Values: -24.0..24.0 dB, 0 = off
  • Input: decimal, up to 3 places, . or ,

A positive value keeps bands up to N dB below their threshold, at the coarsest step, instead of zeroing them. A negative value zeroes bands that clear their threshold by less than N dB after the first bit estimate, so the second estimate gives those bits to the rest. PNS and IS bands are left alone; on side bands of an MS pair --side-knee wins.

Examples:

  • Mild, keep more: --band-knee 3.
  • Strong, keep more: --band-knee 6. The useful maximum: above about 9 dB the rescued coarse bands cost so many bits that the rate loop cuts elsewhere.
  • Exaggerated, keep more: --band-knee 24. Worse than 6, measured.
  • Mild, drop more: --band-knee -2.
  • Strong, drop more: --band-knee -6.
  • Exaggerated, drop more: --band-knee -24. Only clearly audible bands stay.

--quant-round <x>

Move the rounding point of the quantizer: fewer or more small spectral lines become zero.

  • Applies to: AAC core. Off by default (stock 0.405).
  • Values: 0.0..0.5
  • Input: decimal, up to 3 places, . or ,

The quantizer rounds each spectral line to a whole step. Stock rounds with an offset of about 0.405, slightly towards zero, which saves bits. 0.5 rounds to nearest: fewer small lines vanish, it costs bits. Lower values send more lines to zero and are cheaper.

Examples:

  • Mild, finer: --quant-round 0.45.
  • Strong, finer: --quant-round 0.5. Round to nearest.
  • Mild, cheaper: --quant-round 0.35.
  • Strong, cheaper: --quant-round 0.25. Quiet details disappear.
  • Exaggerated, cheaper: --quant-round 0.0. Truncation: every line is rounded down.

--pns-above <Hz>

From this frequency up, replace every long-block band by noise of the right level, whatever its tonality.

  • Applies to: AAC-LC, long blocks. Off by default.
  • Values: 1000..48000 Hz
  • Input: whole number

A high band "like SBR, without SBR": the band keeps its level, its content becomes noise. It turns PNS on even below its bitrate gate; --pns 0 still wins. Silent bands stay silent. At low bitrates use it with --pns-budget 1 and --pns-shared 1, otherwise frames can run out of bits and crackle (see --pns-budget).

Examples:

  • Mild: --pns-above 11000. Only the very top becomes noise.
  • Strong: --pns-above 5000.
  • Exaggerated: --pns-above 2200. Most of the presence region is noise.

--thr-hold <n>

Re-measure the masking model only every n long frames.

  • Applies to: AAC core, long blocks. Off by default.
  • Values: 1..32 frames, 1 = off
  • Input: whole number

Between measurements each band keeps its last noise-to-signal offset, so the noise lags the music. A short block resets the hold. The effect is weak: the stream changes, but measured quality moves by less than 1 dB even at 32, because FDK already works on smoothed energies.

Examples:

  • Mild: --thr-hold 2.
  • Strong: --thr-hold 8.
  • Exaggerated: --thr-hold 32.

--scf-range <n>

Limit how far apart the finest and the coarsest band of a channel may be.

  • Applies to: AAC core. Off by default (stock 60).
  • Values: 0..60, steps of 1.5 dB
  • Input: whole number

Lower values make the quantization noise flatter across frequency, with less psychoacoustic shaping. On ordinary material the effect is close to zero; it shows at small values on material with a strong spectral tilt (6 to 14 kHz noise 12.4 to 7.1 dB below the signal at 0, 64 kbps without PNS). At 14 kbps it does not change the budget.

Examples:

  • Mild: --scf-range 40.
  • Strong: --scf-range 20.
  • Exaggerated: --scf-range 0. One step for the whole channel.

--bit-stats

Count where the bits of the written frames go, and print the table at the end.

  • Applies to: every profile. Off by default.
  • Values: none, a flag

The table splits the frames into transport header, element headers, MS mask, section data, scalefactors, PNS noise levels, TNS, spectrum per channel (mid and side for MS), SBR/PS and fill, plus coded, zeroed, PNS and IS bands per frame. It counts the bits really written; the stream is identical with and without the switch, and the sum matches the ADTS file.

Example: fdkaac -p 2 -b 14000 --bit-stats -f 2 -o out.aac in.wav

--enfac-patch <0|1>

Switch the energy re-weighting of flat, noise-like long frames off, or on for every frame.

  • Applies to: AAC core, long blocks, CBR and VBR. Off by default (stock decides).
  • Values: 0 = never, 1 = every long frame

When the audible spectrum of a frame is flat, stock FDK re-weights band energies (powers 3/4, 1/2 or 1/4) and so moves bits between bands. 0 treats every frame as tonal, 1 applies the re-weighting to every long frame. In CBR this is a fine knob: zeroed bands move by at most 1 point and noise by less than 0.1 dB at 14 to 32 kbps; 0 is often identical to stock on tonal material. In VBR (-m 3) 1 made a tone plus noise file 29% larger and 0 a dense noise file 3% smaller.

F. Block switching and bitrate control

Six switches: one that biases the long/short block decision, and five that control how freely the bitrate may move from frame to frame. The bitrate five only make sense once you know how the engine underneath works, so that comes first.

The CBR engine. You select a target with -b and the encoder aims at it as an average. Frames are not individually forced to that size. At 48 kHz an AAC-LC frame covers 1024 samples, about 21.3 ms, so a 128 kbps stereo target works out to roughly 2730 bits per frame on average. That is the number the rate loop steers towards.

The bit reservoir. Even in CBR, a buffer sits between the frames and the target. When a frame is easy, the encoder codes it sparingly and puts the unspent bits into the reservoir. When a hard frame arrives, it borrows from the reservoir and spends more than its share. The average is preserved while the instantaneous bitrate moves up and down, which is why CBR AAC is never perfectly flat. The reservoir size decides how far the encoder can stray and for how long.

The hard ceiling. An AAC frame holds at most 6144 bits per channel, so 12288 bits for stereo. Nothing in this encoder lifts that; it is a limit of the format. Every reservoir and peak setting is clamped against it, which is also why the swings available here are moderate compared with a true variable bitrate encoder.

Quasi-VBR. The problem these switches address is specific and you have probably heard it: a difficult passage arrives, the encoder releases a few frames well above the average, and then it chokes the following frames to pay the debt back immediately. The result is an audible dimming right after a loud moment. Widening the reservoir and allowing a short term peak lets the encoder hold the elevated quality longer and come back down gently instead. It is still the CBR engine with a bigger buffer, not content based allocation like LAME's, so expect a lighter touch than the name VBR suggests.

A note on the two hard per frame limits: do not set --max-bits-frame below the average or --min-bits-frame above it. Both of those fight the target the rate loop is trying to hit, and the result is worse quality rather than a different tradeoff. --verbose prints the reservoir budget block, including the average bits per frame and the ceilings, which is where you get the numbers to set these switches from.

--block-bias <n>

Sets the share of short blocks, from long blocks only (0) to short blocks only (255). The stream stays standards compliant at every value.

  • Applies to: AAC-LC and the LC core of HE-AAC, which have short blocks. LD and ELD have none (LD switches to a low-overlap window instead, also moved by this switch; ELD has no switching at all).
  • Values: 0..255, 128 is stock; default: -1, off (stock)
  • Input: whole number

A long block gives good frequency resolution and bad time resolution; a short block the reverse. The encoder's attack detector decides frame by frame: it compares the energy of each eighth of the frame with the running energy before it and calls an attack above a ratio of 10. On music that ratio is 1.2 to 1.5 in an ordinary frame and above 10 in only about 2..3% of frames.

This switch moves that threshold along a table calibrated on the encoder itself (six excerpts of acoustic stereo, eurodance, vocals and drums at 64 kbps, share of short frames read from the bitstream), so that the share of short frames follows the slider evenly:

  • 0: long blocks only, no attack is ever detected.
  • 1..127: fewer short frames, down from stock; at 64 about half of the stock share.
  • 128: stock, bit for bit.
  • 129..240: more short frames, about 12% of the frames per 16 steps on music; around 192 about half the frames, at 240 about 80..99%.
  • 241..254: also quiet passages go short (the detector's minimum energy is lowered).
  • 255: short blocks only, every frame, silence included.

Material without transients reacts later than music: steady pink noise and a sine stay long up to about 208 and go short between 216 and 236, because their ratio stays near 1. Material with hard hits only (a drum stem) keeps its hits at every value, so its share moves less. Up to 1.4.0-beta.1 the switch scaled the threshold linearly: above 128 it only went from 10 to 5 (6.5% short at 255 on eurodance) and just below 128 it jumped past most real attacks, which is why the middle seemed to do nothing.

Only the attack decision changes. The window sequence is still built by the encoder's own transitions (long, start, short, stop, long) and both channels are synchronised as stock, so a decoder always gets a legal sequence. Checked: 300 encodes (0 to 255, LC, HE-AAC, HE-AAC v2, short grouping 1 and 3, silence, sine, music), 0 illegal window transitions and 0 decoder errors. --verbose shows the value.

Examples (eurodance, 64 kbps; share of short frames):

  • --block-bias 64: 0.1% instead of 1.6%. Steady material stays long, hard hits still go short.
  • --block-bias 160: 23%. Every larger rise in level gets short blocks.
  • --block-bias 192: 57%.
  • --block-bias 224: 87%.
  • --block-bias 255: 100%. Tonal material sounds rough, a demonstration of what long blocks are for.
  • --block-bias 0: 0%. Every transient is carried by a long block, pre-echo becomes the main artefact on percussive material.

Works with / against: with --block-bias 0, --preecho-rise, --preecho-depth and --tns-thresh from group E are the tools left against pre-echo, the situation LD and ELD are always in. --short-groups, --window-shape and --pns-short only matter where short blocks occur. --attack-decay and --attack-floor change the detector itself; they combine with this switch.

--vbr-reservoir <bits>

Sets the size of the bit reservoir, which decides how far and how long a frame may stray from the average.

  • Applies to: every profile, in CBR mode. This is the central quasi-VBR knob.
  • Values: bits, from 1 up to 6144 * channels minus the average bits per frame, and clamped there automatically; default: -1, off
  • Input: whole number

The reservoir is the buffer the encoder borrows from and pays into. Its size is the memory of the system: a small reservoir means every overspend has to be repaid almost immediately, which produces the dimming right after a climax; a large one means the encoder can stay above the average for several frames and then return gradually.

Bigger is therefore the direction for material that should keep living after a difficult passage. Smaller holds the average more tightly from moment to moment, which is what you want when a transport link has a narrow instantaneous budget. A good starting point is two to three times whatever --verbose reports as the default.

Two behaviours to know. 0 is treated as unset and does nothing, so if you want tight tracking of the average use a small positive value or --bitres-mode 2. And any value at or above the ceiling, that is 6144 * channels minus the average bits per frame, collapses to the ceiling, so there is no way to overdo it and no benefit in trying; asking for 100000 bits on a stereo file gets you the same stream as asking for the ceiling exactly.

Read the numbers from --verbose, which prints the average bits per frame and the ceilings in its reservoir budget block. Setting this switch from a guess is how people end up at the ceiling by accident.

Examples:

  • Mild, more breathing: --vbr-reservoir 4000. Slightly wider swings at 128 kbps stereo; the encoder recovers from a peak over a few frames rather than one.
  • Strong, more breathing: --vbr-reservoir 12000. The practical quasi-VBR setting at 128 kbps stereo. Climaxes hold their quality and the return to average is gradual.
  • Exaggerated, more breathing: --vbr-reservoir 100000. Far above the ceiling, so it collapses to the ceiling. The encoder uses the maximum breathing the format allows, and the caricature is instructive precisely because it shows how modest that maximum is: the 6144 bits per channel limit, not the reservoir, is what bounds AAC's flight.
  • Mild, tighter: --vbr-reservoir 2000. Below the usual default. Swings narrow, the average is tracked more closely.
  • Strong, tighter: --vbr-reservoir 500. Very little buffer. Hard frames cannot borrow much, so difficult passages are coded coarsely rather than expensively.
  • Exaggerated, tighter: --vbr-reservoir 1. Effectively no reservoir. Every frame must fit its own share, so transients and dense passages have nowhere to go, which sounds like a worse version of what --bitres-mode 2 does on purpose.

Works with / against: pairs with --peak-bitrate, which raises the short term ceiling the reservoir can push against; the reservoir without a raised peak has less room to act. --bitres-mode can reduce or disable the reservoir regardless of the size set here. --max-bits-frame caps individual frames after all of this, so a tight cap makes a large reservoir pointless. With --unlock-bitrate the reservoir becomes more important, since overshoot is the normal case there.

Measured: 0 is treated as unset and produces no effect. Values at or above 6144 * channels minus the average bits per frame collapse to the ceiling. --verbose prints the reservoir budget block that gives you both numbers.

--peak-bitrate <bps>

Allows short term bitrate peaks up to the value you give while the average is still held at the -b target.

  • Applies to: every profile, in CBR mode.
  • Values: bits per second, above the -b target; default: -1, off
  • Input: whole number

The reservoir decides how long a frame may stray; this decides how high it may go. Set it above your target and the encoder is allowed to let difficult frames reach that rate momentarily, while the rate loop continues to steer the average towards -b.

Below the target it is ignored, which is deliberate: a peak ceiling under the average makes no sense and the encoder does not act on the contradiction. The useful range is therefore above -b only, and the natural way to express it is as a multiple: half again the target is cautious, double is generous.

The ceiling of 6144 bits per channel still applies, so asking for a peak beyond what the frame can physically hold gives you the frame limit instead. At 48 kHz stereo that is about 576 kbps worth of frame (6144 x 2 bits x 48000 / 1024), so for ordinary targets you have plenty of room before the format limit binds.

Examples:

  • Mild, higher peak: -b 128000 --peak-bitrate 150000. A small amount of headroom. Climaxes come out slightly less compressed.
  • Strong, higher peak: -b 128000 --peak-bitrate 200000. The practical setting, usually paired with a wide reservoir. Difficult frames reach well above the target and the average is kept.
  • Exaggerated, higher peak: -b 128000 --peak-bitrate 1100000. Close to the format frame limit. The encoder may spend almost a whole frame's physical capacity on one frame, after which the rate loop must claw all of it back, so you get a dramatic version of the very dimming the quasi-VBR settings exist to prevent.
  • Downward there is no useful direction: any value at or below the -b target is ignored, so --peak-bitrate 100000 with -b 128000 behaves as if the switch were absent.

Works with / against: the natural companion of --vbr-reservoir; both are needed for the "do not choke after a peak" behaviour, since one grants height and the other grants time. --max-bits-frame is applied after this switch, so an explicit frame cap wins over the peak. --bitres-mode 2 makes this irrelevant by removing the reservoir that the peak would be drawn from.

Measured: the tip configuration in --help for quasi-VBR at about 128 kbps stereo is -b 128000 --peak-bitrate 200000 --vbr-reservoir 12000.

--max-bits-frame <bits>

A hard ceiling on the number of bits any single frame may use.

  • Applies to: every profile, in CBR mode.
  • Values: bits, at least the average bits per frame and at most 6144 * channels; default: -1, off
  • Input: whole number

This is the blunt instrument of the group. Where --peak-bitrate expresses a rate that the engine may reach, this expresses an absolute per frame cap that is applied afterwards and wins. If you give both, the explicit cap is what binds.

It is a constraint, not a quality knob, and its main use is meeting an external requirement: a transport that cannot carry a frame above a certain size, or a test that needs the maximum frame bounded. A reasonable cap is around one and a half times the average, which still allows real breathing while bounding the worst case.

Set it too low and loud frames are starved, which is audible as exactly the dimming you were presumably trying to avoid. Below the average it fights the target outright, and the encoder cannot both honour your cap and hit the bitrate you asked for.

Examples:

  • Mild, higher cap: at 128 kbps stereo (about 2730 bits per frame average), --max-bits-frame 8000. Well above anything the engine would normally produce, so effectively a safety net.
  • Strong, higher cap: --max-bits-frame 12288. The stereo format maximum. The cap is present but never binds, which is the right setting when you want the switch documented in a command line without changing behaviour.
  • Exaggerated, higher cap: --max-bits-frame 100000. Above the format limit, so it is not stored as is in the frame: the encoder takes the smaller of it and its own reservoir maximum (6144 * channels), so the stream is identical to 12288 in stereo (measured: 12288, 20000 and 100000 give the same ADTS stream). Harmless, and a useful reminder that no switch here lifts the frame limit.
  • Mild, lower cap: --max-bits-frame 4000 at 128 kbps stereo. Peaks trimmed, average untouched; close to the useful 1.5x guideline.
  • Strong, lower cap: --max-bits-frame 3000 at the same target. Loud frames are clearly starved, since the cap sits barely above the average.
  • Exaggerated, lower cap: --max-bits-frame 2000 at 128 kbps stereo, below the average. The cap and the target now contradict each other every frame; quality drops across the board and nothing is gained, which is why the rule is to stay at or above the average.

Works with / against: applied after --peak-bitrate, so the explicit cap wins when both are given. A tight cap makes a large --vbr-reservoir pointless, since the reservoir's bits have nowhere to be spent. Read the average bits per frame from the --verbose reservoir budget block before choosing a value.

Measured: AAC-LC 128 kbps on attacks48. Stock largest frame is 3816 payload bits. With --max-bits-frame 3000 the largest becomes 2888; with 4000 it becomes 3440.

--min-bits-frame <bits>

A hard floor on the number of bits every frame must use.

  • Applies to: every profile, in CBR mode.
  • Values: bits, from 0 up to the average bits per frame; default: -1, off
  • Input: whole number

The mirror of the ceiling, and the less useful half. A floor forces the encoder to spend at least this much on every frame, including frames that genuinely need less, such as silence or a sustained quiet passage. Those bits are not saved for later, they are spent, so the floor converts easy frames into wasted budget.

The reason it exists is constant rate transport and experiments: some downstream equipment prefers a predictable minimum frame, and sometimes you want to see what the quantiser does when it is told to spend more than it wants.

Keep it at 0 unless you have such a reason. Above the average it fights the target the same way a too low ceiling does, and the encoder cannot honour both.

Examples:

  • Mild, higher floor: --min-bits-frame 1000 at 128 kbps stereo. Silent and very easy frames get padded; the average has to be recovered from the harder frames.
  • Strong, higher floor: --min-bits-frame 2000 at the same target, close to the average. Almost every frame is now floored, so the engine loses most of its ability to redistribute.
  • Exaggerated, higher floor: --min-bits-frame 2730 at 128 kbps stereo, that is the average itself. Combined with the average as a target, this is close to forcing a rigid per frame rate, except that --bitres-mode 2 does that properly and without fighting the rate loop.
  • Mild, lower floor: --min-bits-frame 200. Barely a constraint; easy frames stay cheap.
  • Strong, lower floor: --min-bits-frame 1. Effectively no floor, the same intent as leaving the switch off.
  • Exaggerated, lower floor: --min-bits-frame 0. The documented off value for the floor; identical in effect to not setting it, and the setting to return to when an experiment is over.

Works with / against: the opposite end of --max-bits-frame, and the two are usually discussed together as constrained VBR. A high floor wastes the bits that --vbr-reservoir exists to redistribute, so the two work against each other. --bitres-mode 2 is the correct way to get rigid per frame behaviour.

Measured: AAC-LC 128 kbps on attacks48. Stock output 97056 bytes; with --min-bits-frame 1000 97111 bytes; with 2900 97709 bytes.

--bitres-mode <n>

Selects how much of the bit reservoir mechanism is active, from full breathing to a rigid per frame bitrate.

  • Applies to: every profile, in CBR mode.
  • Values: 0 full, 1 reduced, 2 disabled; default: -1, leave it to the encoder
  • Input: whole number

Where --vbr-reservoir sets the size of the buffer, this selects the policy. Full is the normal behaviour and the same as stock. Reduced narrows the reservoir's operation. Disabled removes it, so each frame has to fit its own share of the budget, which is the closest this encoder gets to a rigid frame by frame constant bitrate.

The one clearly useful setting is 2, and the use case is a transport or a test that needs each frame to be about the same size. The cost is real: a transient or a dense passage cannot borrow, so it is coded coarsely instead.

Values:

  • -1: leave it to the encoder (default).
  • 0: full reservoir, the same as stock behaviour.
  • 1: reduced reservoir. On our material this gave the same stream as 2, so treat the distinction between the two as untested rather than established.
  • 2: reservoir disabled. Frames become near constant in size and difficult passages lose their escape route.

Example in context:

fdkaac -p 2 -b 128000 --bitres-mode 2 -o rigid.m4a in.wav

Works with / against: 2 makes --vbr-reservoir and --peak-bitrate irrelevant, since there is no reservoir for them to act on. 0 is the mode in which the rest of group F is meant to be used.

Measured: AAC-LC 128 kbps on attacks48. 0 gives the stock stream; 1 and 2 gave the same stream as each other, so no difference between reduced and disabled was observed on that material.

G. DAB+ output

Two switches, and a mode rather than a tweak. Everywhere else in this reference the encoder makes a file or a bare AAC stream; with --dab it makes a DAB+ superframe stream for terrestrial digital radio.

--dab

Emits a DAB+ superframe bitstream per ETSI TS 102 563 instead of a file or a plain AAC stream.

  • Applies to: AAC-LC, HE-AAC and HE-AAC v2, mono or stereo, at 32000 or 48000 Hz. Replaces the normal output path entirely, so -f and the container options do not apply.
  • Values: flag; default: off (normal file or stream output)
  • Input: flag

DAB+ does not simply carry AAC in a different container. Three things differ. The transform is 960 samples rather than the usual 1024, so the frame length fits the radio timing grid. The audio is packed into superframes of exactly 120 ms. And because the air is hostile, with fading, tunnels and interference, the bitstream is protected twice: a firecode, which is a Fire CRC, guards the header, and Reed-Solomon RS(120,110) over GF(256) protects the payload, so a receiver can rebuild data that arrived damaged. The encoder does all of that.

The output is a raw .dabp stream, one superframe after another and nothing else. It is not a file for a media player; it is what a DAB+ multiplexer ingests. The usual chain is out.dabp into odr-dabmux, which combines several stations into one ensemble and emits ETI, and from there to a transmitter or to a software receiver such as welle.io or dablin for testing.

Three constraints the standard imposes and the encoder enforces: the sample rate must be 32000 or 48000 Hz, nothing else is legal in DAB+; the bitrate must be a multiple of 8 kbps between 8 and 192; mono and stereo are both fine.

The bitrate convention here is its own. With --dab, -b is kilobits per second and the encoder multiplies by 1000 itself, so -b 96 means 96 kbps. --unlock-bitrate does not change that: the DAB+ check and the x1000 run first, so --dab --unlock-bitrate -b 96 is the same stream as without the unlock, and -b 96000 is rejected as not a multiple of 8 kbps.

You normally do not choose the profile. DAB+ practice, following the reference tool odr-audioenc, derives it from bitrate and channel count so that low bitrates automatically get the more economical tools:

  • stereo at 48 kbps or below, that is a subchannel size of 6 or less, gives HE-AAC v2 with parametric stereo;
  • mono up to 64 kbps, or stereo up to 80 kbps, gives HE-AAC with SBR;
  • anything higher gives AAC-LC.

So -b 96 on a stereo file lands on AAC-LC, -b 64 on HE-AAC and -b 32 on HE-AAC v2 without you thinking about it. You can still force the profile with -p (2 for LC, 5 for HE-AAC, 29 for HE-AAC v2) when you know what you want. A forced profile gives the same stream as the automatic pick would for that profile, bit for bit. Other -p values are refused, and so is -p 29 on a mono input. A forced profile below the FDK tuning tables, for example -p 2 at 8 kbps or -p 5 at 8 kbps stereo, stops with a note: leave out -p or add --unlock-bitrate.

Values:

  • omitted: normal output, and the encoder behaves exactly as stock.
  • --dab: DAB+ superframe output.

Examples in context:

fdkaac --dab -b 96 -o out.dabp input.wav     # auto AAC-LC
fdkaac --dab -b 64 -o out.dabp input.wav     # auto HE-AAC
fdkaac --dab -b 32 -o out.dabp input.wav     # auto HE-AAC v2

Works with / against: --dab-label only does something in this mode. -f and the MP4 container switches are inapplicable, since there is no container. --unlock-bitrate changes how -b is read. The switches of groups B to F still act on the AAC core inside the superframes, so the stereo, SBR, detail, transient, coverage and bitrate knobs remain available. Frequencies given in Hz are converted on the 960 sample grid, so --verbose shows slightly different band edges than in a 1024 sample stream. Measured on music and on an attack test, 48 and 96 kbps LC, 64 kbps HE-AAC stereo, 48 kbps HE-AAC mono and 32 kbps HE-AAC v2: 58 settings of the switches added since v1.3.2 and of the band switches, each stream decoded by faad2 behind dablin without an error. A switch whose tool is not in the chosen profile does nothing, as in a normal stream: SBR knobs in AAC-LC, stereo knobs in mono and in HE-AAC v2.

Measured: the streams decode with the independent faad2 decoder behind dablin, the DLS label reads back correctly, and the AAC-LC output is bit identical to what the reference odr-audioenc produces from the same input. Nine combinations were tested, 48 and 32 kHz against mono and stereo across the three profiles, each independently decodable.

--dab-label <text>

Carries a static station label in the superframe, shown on a DAB+ receiver's display.

  • Applies to: --dab mode only. Without --dab the label is ignored, because there is no superframe to put it in.
  • Values: text, up to about 48 characters; default: no label
  • Input: text string

A DAB+ receiver shows text on its display, usually the station name and often the title of what is playing. That text travels in a channel called DLS, the Dynamic Label Segment, tucked into the superframe as X-PAD data. This encoder carries a static label: one fixed string for the whole file or stream.

Static is the operative word. The text does not change over the course of the encode. A truly dynamic label that updates track by track, and the MOT slideshow that puts artwork on the screen, are the job of ODR-PadEnc in the OpenDigitalRadio chain and are not part of this encoder.

The length limit of about 48 characters comes from three DLS segments packed into one PAD. Receivers vary in how much of a long label they display at once, so for a station name the useful length is shorter than the limit.

Values:

  • omitted: no label in the stream.
  • --dab-label <text>: the given string is carried as DLS. Quote it if it contains spaces.

Example in context:

fdkaac --dab -b 96 --dab-label "Radio DHT" -o out.dabp input.wav

Works with / against: requires --dab. Independent of every other switch, since it touches the PAD and not the audio.

Appendices

Reading --verbose

--verbose prints the values the encoder actually initialised, before encoding starts. The block comes in sections. Most fields name themselves; the ones below need a word.

Field How to read it
AOT (profile) 2 AAC-LC, 5 HE-AAC, 29 HE-AAC v2, 23 AAC-LD, 39 AAC-ELD.
bitrate-mode 0 CBR, 1..5 VBR (higher = better).
channel-mode 1 mono, 2 stereo. In HE-AAC v2 the core is mono and PS makes the stereo.
core bandwidth Upper edge of the AAC core, snapped to the nearest scalefactor band edge, so it can differ from what you typed (-w 17300 gives 17915 Hz). The bracket names the source: from -w, from --core-cutoff or auto. Under SBR this is only the core; SBR continues above it. Under SBR -w is ignored, although the source may still read from -w.
final BW (AAC+SBR) Only with SBR: the approximate top of the whole signal, core plus SBR, derived from the SBR stop index.
signaling-mode How SBR and PS are signalled: 0 implicit, 1 explicit backward compatible, 2 explicit hierarchical.
SBR ratio 1 downsampled (single rate), 2 dual rate (the core runs at half the sample rate).
sbr amp res Envelope amplitude resolution: 0 = 1.5 dB, 1 = 3.0 dB.
granule length, frame length Samples per frame: 1024 for LC, 512 or 480 for LD and ELD.
codec delay Delay in samples per channel, total and core only. Matters for gapless playback.
active SFBs (long) Number of scalefactor bands in a long block. The ceiling for --msbands, --isbands and every <sfb> argument.
IS corr threshold, IS L/R ratio thresh Q8 values (256 = 1.0). A lower correlation threshold makes intensity stereo more eager.
IS min contiguous SFBs How many adjacent bands must qualify before intensity stereo starts.
TNS mask Bit mask 0x0..0xF of the active TNS filters.
MS/IS bands: auto up to N Top band index up to which the tool may act.
sbr header period Frames and milliseconds between SBR headers: how long a decoder that tunes into a stream waits for SBR. For ELD it reports the LATM/LOAS configuration period, as ELD has no in-band SBR header.
bit reservoir budget Average bits per frame and the reservoir ceilings for mono and stereo. Use it to choose --vbr-reservoir.
franken overrides applied The switches that deviate from stock in this run, with the effective value. If a switch you gave is missing here, it did not take effect.

Scalefactor bands and frequencies

Bands are numbered from 0 at the bottom. The table shows the approximate upper edge of every fourth band in a long block, for each sample rate; the last row gives the number of bands and the Nyquist frequency. Use it to translate --msbands, --isbands, --hole-start and the other <sfb> arguments into hertz. The exact count for your run is in --verbose (active SFBs (long)), and it also depends on the coded bandwidth.

SFB 16 kHz 22.05 kHz 32 kHz 44.1 kHz 48 kHz 96 kHz
0 62 43 62 86 94 188
4 312 215 312 431 469 938
8 562 388 562 775 844 1688
12 875 646 1000 1378 1500 2438
16 1250 991 1500 2067 2250 3750
20 1656 1335 2250 3101 3375 5625
24 2188 1852 3375 4651 5062 8062
28 2875 2584 5000 6891 7500 12938
32 3844 3618 7000 9647 10500 24000
36 5188 5039 9000 12403 13500 36000
40 7000 7020 11000 15159 16500 48000
44 none 9647 13000 17916 19500 none
48 none none 15000 22050 24000 none
number of bands / Nyquist 43 / 8000 47 / 11025 51 / 16000 49 / 22050 49 / 24000 41 / 48000

AAC-LC: automatic core bandwidth by bitrate

When you give no -w, FDK picks the bandwidth from a table indexed by bitrate per channel, and then snaps it to a scalefactor band edge. Measured with --verbose at 48 kHz (stereo -b is the total for both channels):

-b stereo core bandwidth -b mono core bandwidth
24000, 32000 6843 Hz 12000, 16000 5062 Hz
40000, 48000 9750 Hz 20000, 24000 7500 Hz
56000, 64000 13500 Hz 28000, 32000 9750 Hz
80000, 96000 14250 Hz 40000, 48000 12750 Hz
128000 15750 Hz 64000 14250 Hz
192000 17250 Hz 96000 17250 Hz

A higher -w than this only helps if there are bits for it: at 24..50 kbps a forced wide band is exactly where group E (holes) comes in. Above 20 kHz the core needs --uncap-bandwidth and a sample rate that has room for it.

The M4A container: what is mandatory and what can go

An .m4a file is a tree of boxes. The playback skeleton is mandatory and has no switch: ftyp, mdat (the audio data) and moov with mvhd, trak, tkhd, mdia (mdhd, hdlr, minf with smhd, dinf and stbl, which holds stsd, stts, stsc, stsz, stco). Without it no decoder can find the audio.

Optional is the whole udta / meta / ilst block: the encoder identification tag (©too), iTunSMPB (encoder delay for gapless playback) and all text tags.

Switch Works on What it does
--no-tool-tag .m4a Leaves out the encoder identification tag. Other tags and gapless data stay.
--minimal-moov .m4a The smallest legal file: leaves out the whole metadata block (encoder tag, iTunSMPB, all tags). The playback skeleton stays.

Measured on 2 s of 128 kbps stereo: 34381 bytes by default, 34297 with --no-tool-tag (84 less), 34048 with --minimal-moov (333 less). The overhead is mostly the mandatory skeleton. For no container at all, write raw ADTS (-f 2 -o out.aac), which has no tags and no gapless data.

--minimal-moov removes iTunSMPB, so joined tracks may get tiny gaps because the encoder delay is no longer signalled. For albums meant to play without gaps, keep the default.

Default output against the original binary

Checked against the original binary supplied with dBpoweramp R17 (the same libfdk-aac 4.0.1, package 2.0.3). AAC-LC is bit identical (same md5). HE-AAC and HE-AAC v2 differ in bytes but not in quality: the HE-AAC v2 spectrum matches to 0.000 dB, and the HE-AAC error relative to the original is the same as the original's own (7.05e11 against 7.05e11). The byte differences come from a different compiler (mingw/gcc against MSVC) and fixed point rounding.

Building and testing

Building

The repository contains sources only. GitHub Actions builds both Windows binaries on request (workflow "Build Franken FDK AAC (Windows x64 + x86)", button "Run workflow") and for every release tag. To build locally on Linux or WSL with mingw-w64 and autotools:

bash tools/build_win.sh both      # or x64 / x86

The script builds each architecture from a fresh copy of the sources, the same way CI does, and leaves fdkaac-franken-x64.exe and fdkaac-franken-x86.exe in the project directory. All changes to libfdk-aac go through one module, libAACenc/src/franken.{h,cpp} (the global g_franken block, where every unset value means "stock"); the new library parameters (AACENC_PARAM, range 0xF0xx) are declared in aacenc_lib.h.

Tests

make check

runs tests/check.sh on the built binaries. It checks that every switch appears in --help, that every switch produces a stream the decoders accept without errors, that each switch really changes the stream on material where it should act, that values read from the bitstream match what was requested (window shape, groups, frame sizes), that holes and pre-echo move in the measured direction after decoding, and that without the new switches the output is byte identical to stock. It needs ffmpeg and python3; exit code 0 means all passed, 1 failures, 77 a missing dependency.

The measuring tools used for the numbers in this document are in tools/: tools/core/ (holes and pre-echo after decoding), tools/invf/ (SBR inverse filtering and low bitrate floors), tools/msis/ (MS and IS read from the bitstream, SBR headers, stream tune-in), tools/dab/ (DAB+ verification).

PL Wersja polska

Franken FDK AAC

Franken FDK AAC to enkoder Fraunhofer FDK AAC (libfdk-aac 2.0.3, mstorsjo/fdk-aac) z frontendem nu774/fdkaac 1.0.2, rozszerzony o przełączniki odsłaniające decyzje, które FDK zwykle podejmuje sam: jak kodowane jest stereo, jak odtwarzane jest górne pasmo, gdzie wolno wyzerować pasma, jak traktowane są transjenty, jak bity rozkładają się w czasie. Jest przeznaczony dla realizatorów, którzy chcą usłyszeć i zmierzyć, co te decyzje robią, zwłaszcza w skrajnościach: przy bardzo niskim bitrate'ie, wymuszonym szerokim paśmie, mowie i streamingu.

Autor: Michał Dziwisz. Konsultant merytoryczny: Patryk Faliszewski. Zbudowany na otwartym kodzie: libfdk-aac (Fraunhofer IIS) i frontend nu774/fdkaac.

Bez żadnego z nowych przełączników enkoder daje dokładnie to samo co stockowy FDK, bajt w bajt. Każdy przełącznik trzeba włączyć świadomie i każdy może też pogorszyć wynik: pozwalają wejść tam, gdzie strojenie FDK celowo nie wchodzi.

Pobieranie

Gotowe binarki dla Windows są publikowane jako GitHub Releases i pobierają się bez konta:

https://github.com/michaldziwisz/franken-fdk-aac/releases/latest

Wersje beta (na przykład v1.4.0-beta.1) są publikowane jako wydania wstępne, więc powyższy odnośnik nadal pokazuje ostatnią wersję stabilną; bety są na pełnej liście: https://github.com/michaldziwisz/franken-fdk-aac/releases

franken-fdk-aac-x64-vX.Y.Z.zip to wersja 64-bitowa, x86 to 32-bitowa. Każde archiwum zawiera statyczny enkoder (bez bibliotek DLL) i pełną dokumentację. Samo repozytorium ma tylko źródła; budowanie opisuje koniec tego dokumentu.

Pierwsze kroki

Wszystkie oryginalne opcje frontendu działają jak dotąd (-p, -b, -m, -w, -a, -s, -f, znaczniki); fdkaac-franken-x64.exe --help wypisuje je razem z nowymi.

  1. Zakoduj raz z --verbose i przeczytaj, co enkoder faktycznie ustawił:

    fdkaac-franken-x64.exe -p 2 -b 128000 --verbose -o out.m4a in.wav

  2. Zmieniaj jeden przełącznik naraz i sprawdzaj w --verbose, w sekcji franken overrides applied, czy zadziałał.

  3. Słuchaj, a gdzie to ważne, mierz: zdekoduj obie wersje i porównaj. Narzędzia w katalogu tools/ repozytorium robią to dla dziur, pre-echa i błędu stereo.

Przepisy

Każdy z nich to sprawdzony punkt wyjścia, a nie preset do stosowania w ciemno.

# LC stereo przy niskim bitrate'ie z szerokim pasmem: mniej dziur, mniej pre-echa
fdkaac-franken-x64.exe -p 2 -b 32000 -w 17000 --hole-ladder 2 --avoid-holes 1 --window-shape 1 -o out.m4a in.wav

# To samo przy 40 kbps, ze spokojniejszym detektorem ataków dla perkusji
fdkaac-franken-x64.exe -p 2 -b 40000 -w 18000 --hole-ladder 2 --avoid-holes 1 --window-shape 1 --attack-decay 0,9 -o out.m4a in.wav

# Szersze stereo kosztem środka
fdkaac-franken-x64.exe -p 2 -b 96000 --side-bias 6 -o out.m4a in.wav

# Quasi-VBR wokół 128 kbps: silnik CBR z miejscem na oddech
fdkaac-franken-x64.exe -p 2 -b 128000 --peak-bitrate 160000 --vbr-reservoir 6000 -o out.m4a in.wav

# HE-AAC z łagodniejszym, mniej metalicznym górnym pasmem
fdkaac-franken-x64.exe -p 5 -b 32000 --sbr-softness 4 -o out.m4a in.wav

# HE-AAC v2 z szerszym rdzeniem, niż wybiera FDK
fdkaac-franken-x64.exe -p 29 -b 48000 --core-cutoff 7500 -o out.m4a in.wav

# Strumień, który słuchacz dołączający w trakcie od razu dekoduje z SBR
fdkaac-franken-x64.exe -p 5 -b 48000 --sbr-header-period 1 -f 2 -o out.aac in.wav

# AAC-LD z szerszym rdzeniem i częstszym TNS przeciw pre-echu (LD wymaga LOAS, nie ADTS)
fdkaac-franken-x64.exe -p 23 -b 64000 --core-cutoff 12000 --tns-thresh 0,7 -f 10 -o out.loas in.wav

# Skrajnie niski HE-AAC v2: 8000 bps stereo
fdkaac-franken-x64.exe -p 29 -b 8000 --unlock-bitrate -o out.m4a in48k.wav

# Pełny rdzeń 42 kHz ze źródła 96 kHz
fdkaac-franken-x64.exe -p 2 -b 320000 --uncap-bandwidth --core-cutoff 40000 -o out.m4a in96k.wav

Ostatni przepis daje w --verbose core bandwidth: 42000 Hz: żądane 40000 Hz zostaje wyrównane w górę do najbliższej granicy pasma współczynnika skali.

Opis przełączników

To jest opis wszystkich przełączników. Każdy ma własną kartę, a każda karta jest zbudowana tak samo, więc można czytać tylko tę kartę, która jest potrzebna.

Jak czytać kartę

Nagłówek to przełącznik w takiej postaci, w jakiej się go wpisuje, razem z argumentem. Pod nim:

  • Jedno zdanie pogrubione: do czego służy przełącznik, pod kątem dźwięku albo zachowania.
  • Dotyczy: profile i sytuacje, w których przełącznik faktycznie działa. Jeśli w jakimś profilu nie robi nic, jest to napisane właśnie tutaj.
  • Wartości: dozwolony zakres, jednostka i wartość domyślna. Domyślna to zawsze ta, która oznacza „decyduje stock FDK”.
  • Wpisywanie: czy przełącznik przyjmuje liczbę całkowitą, liczbę dziesiętną, flagę czy tekst.
  • Dalej wyjaśnienie: najpierw problem, potem mechanizm w prostych słowach, a na końcu co się dzieje przy zwiększaniu i zmniejszaniu wartości.
  • Przykłady: dla przełącznika liczbowego z miejscem na manewr nastawa łagodna, mocna i celowo przesadzona, w każdym kierunku. Przełączniki wyliczeniowe mają w tym miejscu listę wszystkich wartości.
  • Współpraca i konflikty: zależności oraz informacja, który inny przełącznik przesłania ten.
  • Zmierzone: wyłącznie rzeczywiste pomiary, z materiałem i warunkami. Jeśli pomiaru nie ma, nie ma też tej linii.
  • Pod maską: jedna albo dwie linie dla ciekawych. Można je spokojnie pominąć.

Wspólne zasady

Obowiązują w każdej karcie i nie są w nich powtarzane.

Wyłączony = stock. Każdy przełącznik Frankensteina jest wyłączony, dopóki się go nie poda. Jeśli żadnego z nich nie ma w linii poleceń, wynik jest identyczny bajt w bajt z wynikiem stock FDK (zachowanie niezmienionego FDK). „Wyłączony” zapisuje się jako -1 dla niemal wszystkich (to wartownik oznaczający „zostaw własną decyzję FDK”) oraz jako 0 dla --core-cutoff, --side-bias, --side-knee i --mask-slope, gdzie -1 byłoby poprawną, działającą wartością.

Liczby dziesiętne. Przełączniki dziesiętne przyjmują . albo ,, do trzech miejsc po przecinku. Wartość jest zaokrąglana w połowie od zera, a zakres jest sprawdzany już na wartości zaokrąglonej. Wszystko inne to błąd: 1e1, nan, 3x, spacje w środku. Przełączniki całkowitoliczbowe odrzucają części dziesiętne, więc 128,5 tam, gdzie oczekiwana jest liczba całkowita, jest błędem, a nie cichym obcięciem.

Jednostki. W całym tekście pojawiają się cztery konwencje:

  • dB: zwykłe przesunięcie w decybelach, dodatnie albo ujemne.
  • x100: liczba całkowita będąca stukrotnością wartości rzeczywistej, więc 100 oznacza 1,00, 90 oznacza 0,90, a 400 oznacza 4,00.
  • Q8: liczba całkowita w formacie stałoprzecinkowym, gdzie 256 oznacza 1,0. Czyli 128 to 0,5, 384 to 1,5, 512 to 2,0, a 4096 to 16,0. Q8 to wewnętrzna skala samego FDK i dlatego przecieka ona do linii poleceń.
  • SFB: numer pasma współczynnika skali, liczony od 0 od dołu widma w górę. Pasmo współczynnika skali to grupa linii widmowych, które dzielą jeden współczynnik skali, w przybliżeniu zgodnie z pasmem krytycznym: wąskie na dole, szerokie na górze. Ile tych pasm istnieje, zależy od profilu, częstotliwości próbkowania i kodowanego pasma, więc numer SFB ma sens tylko wtedy, gdy znana jest ta liczba. Wypisuje ją --verbose, na przykład active SFBs (long): 49 przy AAC-LC 48 kHz stereo.

Jak sprawdzić, co weszło w życie. --verbose wypisuje na stderr rzeczywiste, zainicjalizowane parametry kodera, zanim zacznie się kodowanie, a do tego linię franken overrides applied z listą przełączników, które w tym przebiegu naprawdę odbiegają od stock. Jeśli ustawionej wartości tam nie ma, to nie weszła ona w życie i żadne słuchanie nie powie o tym szybciej.

Jak czytany jest -b

Obok siebie żyją trzy konwencje, a pomylenie ich to najczęstszy sposób na zaskakujący rozmiar pliku.

  • Praca normalna: -b to bity na sekundę, ale frontend przyjmuje też postać skróconą. Małe liczby są czytane jako kilobity, więc -b 128 i -b 128000 oznaczają to samo, czyli 128 kbps, a -b 1152 oznacza 1152 kbps.
  • Z --unlock-bitrate: -b jest czytane dosłownie jako bity na sekundę, bez skrótu kilobitowego. -b 6000 to 6000 bps. -b 1152 w tym trybie to 1152 bps, czyli praktycznie nic; program ostrzega, gdy wartość wygląda na taką właśnie pomyłkę.
  • Z --dab: -b to kilobity na sekundę, w zakresie 8..192 i jako wielokrotność 8. Koder sam mnoży przez 1000, więc -b 96 to 96 kbps, także razem z --unlock-bitrate.

Górnego pułapu nie ustawia żaden przełącznik i nie da się go podnieść: ramka AAC mieści najwyżej 6144 bity na kanał. Przy 48 kHz to z grubsza 576 kbps na kanał.

Uwaga o transporcie dla LD i ELD

AAC-LD (-p 23) i AAC-ELD (-p 39) działają w tym koderze, ale nie da się ich zapisać do ADTS. Żądanie -f 2 z tymi profilami kończy się komunikatem „encoder initialization failed”, a winowajcą jest format transportu, nie profil. Trzeba użyć M4A (-f 0, wartość domyślna), LATM (-f 6 albo -f 7) albo LOAS (-f 10). LOAS jest wyborem praktycznym, gdy potrzebny jest goły strumień do pomiaru albo do podania dalej, bo jest to samowystarczalny strumień bajtów, tak jak ADTS dla LC i HE-AAC.

Mapa grup

Przełączniki są pogrupowane w --help z grubsza od łatwych do dziwactw, a ten opis trzyma się tej samej kolejności.

  • A. Na początek: pokrętła dla użytkownika. Diagnostyka, intensity stereo na jednym suwaku, strojenie pod mowę i dwie flagi włączane świadomie, które zdejmują wbudowane ograniczenia.
  • B. Kodowanie stereo: mid/side, intensity stereo w szczegółach, parametric stereo.
  • C. Pasmo i SBR: górne pasmo, jego detektory, estymatory i siatka.
  • D. Maskowanie, kształtowanie szumu i szczegół: progi psychoakustyczne, SNR, którego koder wymaga w każdym pasmie, TNS i PNS.
  • E. Transjenty rdzenia, pre-echo i dziury w widmie: rdzeń LC, LD i ELD pod presją bitów.
  • F. Przełączanie bloków i kontrola bitrate'u: decyzja blok długi czy krótki, rezerwuar bitów i pokrętła quasi-VBR.
  • G. Wyjście DAB+: superramki radia cyfrowego.

A. Na początek

Pięć przełączników. Jeden tylko zagląda do kodera, dwa go stroją, a dwa zdejmują ograniczenia, których pilnuje stock FDK. Jeśli ten build jest dla ciebie nowy, przeczytaj najpierw o --verbose i używaj go po każdym kolejnym przełączniku, który spróbujesz.

--verbose

Wypisuje na stderr rzeczywiste, zainicjalizowane ustawienia kodera przed kodowaniem, żeby było widać, co faktycznie weszło w życie.

  • Dotyczy: każdego profilu i każdego transportu. Nie zmienia nic w strumieniu wyjściowym.
  • Wartości: flaga, bez argumentu; domyślnie: wyłączona
  • Wpisywanie: flaga

Koder podejmuje na podstawie linii poleceń bardzo wiele decyzji i większość z nich nie jest wpisanymi wartościami. Pasmo zostaje przyciągnięte do granicy pasma współczynnika skali. Bitrate wybiera wiersz w tabeli strojenia, a ten z kolei ustawia PNS, intensity stereo i siatkę SBR. Poprawnie zapisany przełącznik może i tak zostać zignorowany, bo profil nie ma takiego mechanizmu. --verbose to jedyny sposób, by zobaczyć wynik tego wszystkiego przed zmarnowaniem czasu na słuchanie.

Co wypisuje: profil (AOT), bitrate i tryb bitrate'u, częstotliwość próbkowania, tryb kanałów, efektywne pasmo rdzenia w Hz wraz ze źródłem tej decyzji (from -w, from --core-cutoff albo auto), stan afterburnera, transport i sygnalizację oraz stan narzędzi kodowania, które koder wybrał sobie sam, czyli TNS, PNS, intensity stereo i MS stereo włączone albo wyłączone. Gdy działa SBR, dochodzi do tego współczynnik SBR, efektywne indeksy częstotliwości początkowej i końcowej, skala częstotliwości, pasma szumu, rozdzielczość amplitudy oraz, dla całego pasma, oszacowanie final BW (AAC+SBR). Wypisuje blok budżetu rezerwuaru, który mówi, ile jest średnio bitów na ramkę i jakie są pułapy, a także active SFBs (long), czyli liczbę potrzebną przed ustawieniem jakiegokolwiek przełącznika opartego na SFB. Na końcu pojawia się lista franken overrides applied, wymieniająca tylko te przełączniki, które w tym przebiegu naprawdę odbiegają od stock.

Trzy odczyty warto obejrzeć uważnie. Pasmo rdzenia jest zakotwiczone w SFB, więc wpisane -w 17300 może wrócić jako 17915 Hz: koder nie potrafi przyciąć w środku pasma. Pod SBR wartość -w jest ignorowana, ale nadal opisana jako [from -w], co dezorientuje do chwili, gdy przypomnimy sobie, że tam działa przełącznik --core-cutoff. A active SFBs (long) to pułap dla --msbands, --isbands i każdego argumentu <sfb>: poproszenie o pasmo 48, gdy kodowanych jest tylko 40, nie jest błędem, po prostu nic nie robi.

Wartości:

  • --verbose: wypisz zrzut. Nie ma przełącznika wyłączającego, bo pominięcie go jest wyłączeniem.

Przykład w kontekście:

fdkaac -p 5 -b 64000 --verbose -o out.m4a in.wav

Współpraca i konflikty: brak. To narzędzie diagnostyczne dla wszystkiego innego w tym opisie i najszybsza odpowiedź na pytanie „czy mój przełącznik w ogóle coś zrobił”.

Zmierzone: przy AAC-LC 48 kHz stereo raportuje active SFBs (long): 49. Przy HE-AAC v2 48 kHz domyślne odcięcie rdzenia wychodzi na 8613 Hz.

--is-aggression <0..100>

Jeden suwak decydujący, jak ochoczo koder zwija górne pasma do intensity stereo, wymieniając separację stereo na górze na bity.

  • Dotyczy: profili z prawdziwym rdzeniem stereo, czyli AAC-LC, AAC-LD i AAC-ELD, oraz rdzenia HE-AAC. Bez efektu w mono. Pod HE-AAC v2 obraz stereo niesie parametric stereo na rdzeniu mono, więc intensity stereo nie ma na czym działać.
  • Wartości: 0..100, gdzie 0 to stock FDK; domyślnie: -1, wyłączone (identycznie jak stock)
  • Wpisywanie: liczba całkowita

Intensity stereo (IS) zastępuje osobne widma lewego i prawego kanału w górnych pasmach jedną wspólną obwiednią energii plus kierunkiem. Ucho słabo lokalizuje wysokie tony, więc jest to tanie: odrzuca się połowę danych w tych pasmach, a płaci separacją stereo na górze. Na materiale, który naprawdę ma różne góry w obu kanałach, na przykład z talerzami ustawionymi mocno na jedną stronę albo z szerokimi efektami przestrzennymi, płaci się słyszalnie.

Stock FDK jest wobec IS wyjątkowo nieśmiały i właśnie dlatego ludzie dochodzą do wniosku, że przełącznik nie robi nic. Bramek jest cztery, a pasmo musi przejść wszystkie naraz: bramka dopuszczenia, która bierze IS pod uwagę tylko wtedy, gdy bitrate na pasmo spada poniżej progu, próg korelacji między kanałami na poziomie około 0,95, minimalna seria 6 kolejnych pasm i stabilny kierunek. Poluzowanie jednej bramki zwykle nie zmienia nic, bo blokuje kolejna. --is-aggression rusza nimi wszystkimi razem, spójnie: usuwa bramkę bitrate'u i pasma oraz, w miarę wzrostu liczby, luzuje wymagania co do korelacji, kierunku i rozmiaru obszaru.

Wyżej oznacza więcej pasm kodowanych jako IS, mniej bitów wydanych na górę widma i słabszą separację stereo właśnie tam. 100 to prawdziwe maksimum: bramka usunięta, korelacja poluzowana do około 0,475, obszar jednego pasma już wystarcza, a tolerancja kierunku jest szeroka. Niskie wartości nie nadają się na delikatną korektę, bo małe poluzowanie często i tak zostawia inną bramkę jako wiążącą; 1 nie zmieniło nic na naszym materiale testowym. Użyteczne okno pracy zaczyna się około 30.

Jest tylko jeden kierunek do przejścia, bo 0 to już stock, a stock jest blisko „nigdy”.

Przykłady:

  • Łagodnie, więcej IS: --is-aggression 30. Na skorelowanym materiale pasma zaczynają trafiać do IS. Obraz na górze zwęża się nieznacznie, kilka procent bitów zostaje uwolnionych na resztę.
  • Mocno, więcej IS: --is-aggression 50. Wyraźnie więcej pasm w IS. Słyszalne zwężenie na talerzach i ogonach pogłosu, realna oszczędność bitów przy 48..96 kbps.
  • Przesadnie, więcej IS: --is-aggression 100. Do IS idzie prawie wszystko, co może. Góra obrazu zlepia się ze środkiem, a spanoramowane góry zaczynają dryfować. Przydatne przy 32 kbps i niżej oraz jako karykatura, żeby usłyszeć, jak brzmi IS puszczone luzem.

Współpraca i konflikty: jawne przełączniki --is-* z grupy B nadpisują to, co ustawił suwak, więc najpierw użyj suwaka, a po --is-corr-thresh, --is-min-sfbs i --is-lr-ratio sięgaj tylko wtedy, gdy potrzebna jest precyzja. --is 0 wyłącza intensity stereo całkowicie i czyni ten przełącznik bez znaczenia. --is-force-lo i --is-force-hi obchodzą każdą bramkę, więc ten suwak nie jest im w ogóle potrzebny. --verbose wypisuje efektywny próg korelacji i minimalną liczbę pasm, czym potwierdza się, że suwak zadziałał.

Zmierzone: na mix48 przy AAC-LC 64 kbps 1 nie dało żadnej zmiany strumienia, a 30, 50 i 100 za każdym razem strumień zmieniły.

--speech

Przełącza warstwę SBR na strojenie pod mowę: inne filtrowanie odwrotne, inny poziom szumu (noise floor) i brak parametrycznego kodowania górnego pasma.

  • Dotyczy: HE-AAC i HE-AAC v2, czyli wszędzie, gdzie działa SBR, także ELD z SBR. Bez efektu w AAC-LC, które nigdzie w FDK nie ma osobnego trybu mowy.
  • Wartości: flaga; domyślnie: wyłączona (strojenie pod muzykę)
  • Wpisywanie: flaga

SBR odbudowuje górne pasmo z zakodowanego pasma dolnego i musi wiedzieć, jak tonalne jest źródło, żeby zdecydować, jak mocno wybielić łatane pasmo i ile szumu dosypać. Muzyka i mowa odpowiadają na to zupełnie inaczej. Mowa przy niskim bitrate cierpi przy strojeniu pod muzykę na konkretny artefakt: odbudowana góra bulgocze, bo estymator wciąż uznaje łatę za tonalną, a w rzeczywistości jest to ruchomy formant plus oddech.

Flaga zmienia kilka rzeczy naraz: tabela decyzyjna filtrowania odwrotnego przełącza się na tabelę mowy, wagowanie poziomu szumu przechodzi na wartość dla mowy, a parametryczne kodowanie górnego pasma zostaje wyłączone. Dla podcastu, audiobooka albo dialogu przy 24..48 kbps efektem jest naturalniejsza góra z mniejszym bulgotaniem.

Jeśli potrzebna jest tylko część tego, co robi flaga, poszczególne elementy są dostępne osobno: --sbr-invf-table 1 daje samą tabelę decyzyjną mowy, --sbr-nf-weight 1.0 samą wagę szumu dla mowy, a --sbr-softness 4 oba te elementy, w każdym przypadku bez ruszania częstotliwości podziału i bez wyłączania kodowania parametrycznego.

Dla mowy w zwykłym AAC-LC nie ma odpowiednika i ten opis nie będzie udawał inaczej. Model psychoakustyczny LC jest ten sam dla mowy i dla muzyki. Pomaga tam natomiast zwężenie pasma, bo mowa ma mało treści wysoko, za pomocą --core-cutoff 12000 do 14000, a przy bardzo niskim bitrate włączenie substytucji szumem przez --pns 1 --force-pns.

Wartości:

  • pominięta: strojenie pod muzykę (stock).
  • --speech: strojenie pod mowę.

Przykład w kontekście:

fdkaac -p 5 -b 32000 --speech -o speech.m4a podcast.wav

Współpraca i konflikty: jawne przełączniki estymatorów SBR z grupy C wygrywają z tą flagą, więc --sbr-invf wymusza ustalony poziom filtrowania odwrotnego bez względu na nią. --sbr-invf-table i --sbr-nf-weight idą za --speech, dopóki zostają na -1, i właśnie tak jedna flaga potrafi ruszyć oba.

Zmierzone: zerowy efekt w AAC-LC; zmienia strumień HE-AAC.

--uncap-bandwidth

Zdejmuje twardy limit 20 kHz, jaki FDK nakłada na pasmo rdzenia, żeby --core-cutoff mógł sięgnąć aż do Nyquista.

  • Dotyczy: każdego profilu z rdzeniem, który da się rozszerzyć, w praktyce AAC-LC przy wysokim bitrate ze źródła o wysokiej częstotliwości próbkowania. Bez --core-cutoff nie ma sensu.
  • Wartości: flaga; domyślnie: wyłączona (obowiązuje limit 20 kHz)
  • Wpisywanie: flaga

FDK ogranicza pasmo rdzenia do min(20000, sr/2). Rozumowanie jest słuszne dla normalnego materiału: powyżej 20 kHz prawie nikt nie słyszy, a bity lepiej wydać niżej. Konsekwencja jest taka, że źródło 96 kHz zakodowane przy hojnym bitrate nadal nie koduje nic powyżej 20 kHz, niezależnie od żądań, a dodatkowa częstotliwość próbkowania kupuje tylko łagodniejsze zbocze filtra antyaliasingowego.

Ta flaga zdejmuje ograniczenie. Sama z siebie nie robi zupełnie nic: podnosi tylko pułap, nie rusza samego pasma. Pasmem rusza przełącznik --core-cutoff <hz>, a ze zdjętym limitem może on sięgnąć do połowy częstotliwości próbkowania.

Warto mieć jasność, co się za to dostaje. Treść powyżej 20 kHz kosztuje bity, które schodzą z pasma słyszalnego, chyba że bitrate ma realny zapas, a strumienie z pasmem poza typowym zakresem leżą poza zwykłą praktyką, więc niektóre dekodery mogą się na nie boczyć. To świadome włączenie i właśnie dlatego jest to osobna flaga, a nie efekt uboczny --core-cutoff.

Wartości:

  • pominięta: pasmo rdzenia ograniczone do 20 kHz (stock).
  • --uncap-bandwidth: ograniczenie zdjęte, --core-cutoff wolny aż do sr/2.

Przykład w kontekście:

fdkaac -p 2 -b 320000 --uncap-bandwidth --core-cutoff 40000 --ath-scale 200 \
    -o out.m4a in96k.wav

Współpraca i konflikty: wymaga --core-cutoff. Pod SBR -w jest ignorowane, a działa --core-cutoff, więc ta para jest też sposobem na rozszerzenie rdzenia siedzącego pod SBR. Naturalnie łączy się z --ath-scale poniżej 256, gdy jest zapas bitrate'u, bo nie ma sensu kodować 40 kHz pasma przy progach strojonych pod niedostatek.

Zmierzone: wejście 96 kHz, AAC-LC 400 kbps, szum szerokopasmowy. Bez flagi --core-cutoff 40000 daje 20000 Hz w --verbose i praktycznie zerową energię powyżej 20 kHz. Z flagą --verbose raportuje 40000 Hz, a zdekodowany plik niesie około 10 procent swojej energii w 20..24 kHz, około 20 procent w 24..32 kHz i około 20 procent w 32..44 kHz. Ten koder nie ma w repozytorium sygnału testowego 96 kHz, więc należy to traktować jako pomiar wcześniejszy, a nie taki, który da się odtworzyć z dostarczonego materiału.

--unlock-bitrate

Zdejmuje dolne progi bitrate'u, których pilnuje każdy profil, i sprawia, że -b to dosłownie bity na sekundę.

  • Dotyczy: każdego profilu. Zdejmowane progi różnią się w zależności od profilu, patrz niżej.
  • Wartości: flaga; domyślnie: wyłączona (wszystkie progi obowiązują)
  • Wpisywanie: flaga

Stock FDK odmawia kodowania poniżej pewnego progu, a próg ten jest inny w każdym profilu i często bywa mylony z ograniczeniem formatu. Nie jest nim. Rozpowszechnione twierdzenie, że HE-AAC nie potrafi zejść poniżej 16 kbps, opisuje najniższy wiersz tabeli strojenia SBR dla 48 kHz stereo i nic więcej. Gdy poprosić o mniej, stock FDK po cichu podnosi bitrate do tego wiersza.

Flaga zdejmuje pięć osobnych progów:

  • minimum rdzenia wynoszące 40 bitów na kanał;
  • regułę LD i ELD o 8 kbps na kanał oraz rezerwę transportową 208 bitów, którą te profile zgadują (rzeczywisty narzut nagłówków jest nadal sprawdzany wobec rzeczywistego kodera transportu);
  • początek tabeli pasma dla LD i ELD, żeby te profile mogły użyć pasma węższego niż pierwszy wiersz tabeli;
  • próg tabeli strojenia SBR i PS: HE-AAC, HE-AAC v2 oraz ELD z SBR poniżej tabeli używają ponownie jej najniższego wiersza, utrzymując tę samą siatkę SBR, podczas gdy żądany bitrate jest respektowany, a rdzeń AAC dostaje po prostu mniej bitów;
  • regułę, że ramka niemogąca pomieścić własnych danych pobocznych przerywa kodowanie. Takie ramki zamiast tego przestrzeliwują, a rezerwuar wchłania to w kolejnych ramkach.

Zostaje nagłówek transportu, 56 bitów na ramkę dla ADTS, oraz górny pułap 6144 bitów na kanał, którego nie rusza żaden przełącznik. Poniżej oszacowanej zawartości SBR, czyli około 2,5 kbps na kanał plus parametric stereo, rdzeń nie miałby już żadnych bitów i koder zatrzymuje się z czystym „encoder initialization failed”, a nie z asercją.

Flaga zdejmuje progi. Nie gwarantuje żadnego konkretnego bitrate'u. Przy takich wartościach gęste fragmenty rutynowo wychodzą powyżej tego, o co poproszono, bo same nagłówki i dane poboczne kosztują więcej niż budżet, więc wynik należy oceniać po rozmiarze pliku, a nie po liczbie w --verbose.

Uwaga na zmianę znaczenia -b. W trybie unlock liczba to dosłownie bity na sekundę. -b 6000 to 6000 bps. -b 6 to 6 bps i nie zakoduje nic sensownego, a -b 1152 wpisane w przekonaniu, że znaczy 1152 kbps, daje 1152 bps. Zawsze trzeba tu wpisywać pełną liczbę bitów.

Wartości:

  • pominięta: wszystkie progi obowiązują, -b czytane ze skrótem kilobitowym (stock).
  • --unlock-bitrate: progi zdjęte, -b to dosłowne bps.

Przykład w kontekście:
